href="#mi-mint-1-ChevronRight"></use></svg></button></div></header><div class="mdx-content @container/columns-container relative mt-8 prose prose-gray dark:prose-invert [contain:inline-size] isolate empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="Configure permissions" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/permissions" id="content"><span data-as="p">The Claude Agent SDK provides permission controls to manage how Claude uses tools. Use permission modes and rules to define what’s allowed automatically, and the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input" class="link"><code>canUseTool</code> callback</a> to handle calls that still need approval.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="how-permissions-are-evaluated" aria-labelledby="_R_39deehkd74t9_"><a href="#how-permissions-are-evaluated" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: How permissions are evaluated"></a><span id="_R_39deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">How permissions are evaluated</span></h2>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">If you load a plugin that contains a <a href="/docs/en/plugins/mods/overview" class="link">mod</a>, see <a href="/docs/en/permissions#precedence-among-rules-hooks-and-mods" class="link">where a mod’s decisions fit among rules and hooks</a>.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">When Claude requests a tool, Claude Code checks the call at these steps. Each step can decide the call, send it to your <code>canUseTool</code> callback, or pass it on:</span>
<div role="list" class="steps ml-3.5 mt-10 mb-6"><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">1</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Hooks</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">Run <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">hooks</a>. A hook can deny the call outright or pass it on. A hook that returns <code>allow</code> doesn’t skip the deny-rule and ask-rule steps, which are evaluated regardless of the hook result.</span><span data-as="p">A <code>PreToolUse</code> hook that returns <code>allow</code> also can’t approve an <code>rm</code> or <code>rmdir</code> command whose target Claude Code recognizes as a <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">critical path</a>, such as <code>rm -rf ~</code>. For what that <code>allow</code> does in plan mode, see <a href="/docs/en/permissions#hook-approvals-in-plan-mode" class="link">Hook approvals in plan mode</a>.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">2</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Deny rules</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">If a deny rule from <code>disallowed_tools</code> or from a <a href="#rules-in-settings-files" class="link">settings file the SDK loads</a> matches, the call is denied and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input" class="link"><code>canUseTool</code></a> isn’t called. The call is denied this way even in <a href="#bypass-permissions-mode-bypasspermissions" class="link"><code>bypassPermissions</code></a> mode and when a hook returned <code>allow</code>.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">3</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Ask rules</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">Check <code>ask</code> rules from <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#permission-settings" class="link">settings.json</a>. If an ask rule matches, the call falls through to your <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input" class="link"><code>canUseTool</code> callback</a> for confirmation, even in <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode.</span><span data-as="p">Tools that require user interaction behave the same way. <code>AskUserQuestion</code> and MCP tools whose server sets <a href="/docs/en/mcp#require-approval-for-a-specific-tool" class="link"><code>_meta[&quot;anthropic/requiresUserInteraction&quot;]</code></a> fall through to the callback, even when an allow rule matches. In <code>dontAsk</code> mode both cases are denied instead, because that mode turns each prompt into a denial. The MCP annotation requires Claude Code v2.1.199 or later.</span><span data-as="p"><a href="/docs/en/mcp#organization-controls-on-connector-tools" class="link">claude.ai connector</a> tools your organization has set to <code>ask</code> also leave the flow at this step. Every call falls through to the callback, even in <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode and even when an allow rule matches. The callback receives the reason <code>Your organization requires approval for this tool</code>. In <code>dontAsk</code> mode the call is denied instead, because that mode never prompts.</span><span data-as="p">In the TypeScript SDK, if you set a custom tool’s <code>permission_policy</code> to <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools#require-approval-for-specific-tools" class="link"><code>always_ask</code></a>, no allow rule approves its calls, not even an <code>allowedTools</code> entry. In <code>dontAsk</code> mode Claude Code denies those calls, and in <code>auto</code>, <code>plan</code>, or <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode it <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#permission-modes-that-can-approve-an-always_ask-call" class="link">can approve them</a> before your callback runs.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">4</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Permission mode</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">Apply the active <a href="#permission-modes" class="link">permission mode</a>:</span><ul>
<li>In <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode, Claude Code approves each call that reaches this step unless it’s one of the <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#actions-no-mode-auto-approves" class="link">actions no mode auto-approves</a>. For which of those actions go to your <code>canUseTool</code> callback, see <a href="#bypass-permissions-mode-bypasspermissions" class="link">Bypass permissions mode</a>.</li>
<li>In <code>acceptEdits</code> mode, Claude Code approves the file operations listed under <a href="#accept-edits-mode-acceptedits" class="link">Accept edits mode</a>.</li>
<li>In <code>plan</code> mode, some calls reach your callback, and when auto mode is available, others go to its classifier instead. For more information, see <a href="#plan-mode-plan" class="link">Plan mode</a>.</li>
<li>In <code>default</code> and <code>dontAsk</code> mode, the call goes on to the allow rules.</li>
<li>For <code>auto</code> mode, see <a href="#auto-mode-auto" class="link">Auto mode</a>.</li>
</ul></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">5</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Allow rules</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">Check <code>allow</code> rules (from <code>allowed_tools</code> and settings.json). If a rule matches, the tool is approved. A call the tool approves on its own is resolved at this step too, with no rule needed: for example a file read inside your working directories or a <a href="/docs/en/permissions#read-only-commands" class="link">read-only Bash command</a>.</span><span data-as="p">Recognized <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">critical-path removals</a> aren’t approved by an allow rule, and commands flagged <code>Dangerous rm operation not ruled out</code> aren’t approved by a <code>Bash</code> or <code>Bash(*)</code> rule. Whether they then reach your callback depends on the permission mode: in an Agent SDK session in <code>auto</code> mode, for example, Claude Code denies them by default without calling it. The <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-path-removals-in-each-permission-mode" class="link">Critical paths mode table</a> lists what each mode does with them.</span><span data-as="p">An allow rule doesn’t approve a read from a <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">network path</a>.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-transparent bg-linear-to-b from-gray-200 dark:from-white/10 via-80% to-transparent [.step-container:has([data-component-part=step-content]:empty)_&amp;]:hidden" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">6</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">canUseTool callback</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">If no earlier step resolves the request, call your <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input" class="link"><code>canUseTool</code> callback</a> for a decision. In <code>dontAsk</code> mode, this step is skipped and the tool is denied, though a <a href="/docs/en/plugins/mods/overview" class="link">mod</a> you installed can still approve <a href="/docs/en/permissions#precedence-among-rules-hooks-and-mods" class="link">some such calls</a>.</span><span data-as="p">In the TypeScript SDK, if you set <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options" class="link"><code>permissionPrompts: &#x27;none&#x27;</code></a>, your callback isn’t called at this step. A <a href="/docs/en/hooks#permissionrequest" class="link"><code>PermissionRequest</code> hook</a> still gets a chance to decide, and if it doesn’t, Claude Code denies the call. The option requires Claude Code v2.1.259 or later.</span></div></div></div></div>
<span data-ramka-trigger="" class="zoom-image-trigger contents cursor-zoom-in"><span class="contents"><picture class="contents"><img data-path="images/agent-sdk/permissions-flow.svg" src="https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/jYgs7qigNjO1Badj/images/agent-sdk/permissions-flow.svg?fit=max&amp;auto=format&amp;n=jYgs7qigNjO1Badj&amp;q=85&amp;s=c771ad9085b1277d3708027a49c744bc" alt="Diagram of the six-step permission evaluation flow matching the steps above: a tool request passes through hooks, deny rules, ask rules, permission mode, allow rules, and canUseTool. in-[a]:hidden"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-4 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Expand"></use></svg></button>
<span data-as="p">If you pass a <code>canUseTool</code> callback in a configuration where the TypeScript SDK expects the evaluation order to auto-approve calls before the callback is consulted, the SDK emits a Node.js process warning once when the query is constructed. The warning’s code is <code>CLAUDE_SDK_CAN_USE_TOOL_SHADOWED</code>. Two configurations trigger it:</span>
<ul>
<li><code>permissionMode: &#x27;bypassPermissions&#x27;</code>: the warning text starts with <code>canUseTool will not be invoked: permissionMode &#x27;bypassPermissions&#x27;</code>. Tool calls are approved before <code>canUseTool</code> is consulted, so your callback runs for only a few calls, such as the calls listed under <a href="#bypass-permissions-mode-bypasspermissions" class="link">Bypass permissions mode</a>. To check the approved calls too, put your callback’s checks in a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link"><code>PreToolUse</code> hook</a></li>
<li>An <code>allowedTools</code> entry that is only a tool name, such as <code>&quot;Read&quot;</code>: the warning lists each such entry after <code>canUseTool will not be invoked for:</code>. Calls to a listed tool are approved before <code>canUseTool</code> is consulted, apart from the <a href="#calls-an-allow-rule-never-approves" class="link">calls an allow rule never approves</a>. To have your callback run for a listed tool’s calls that need approval, remove that tool’s entry from <code>allowedTools</code></li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Entries with a specifier such as <code>Bash(ls *)</code> and the <code>acceptEdits</code> mode don’t trigger it, and allow rules coming from settings files aren’t visible to the check.</span>
<span data-as="p">The warning doesn’t block any call or change any option. If your callback already receives every call you want to check, leave your configuration as it is. To log the warning, listen with <code>process.on(&#x27;warning&#x27;, ...)</code> and match the <code>CLAUDE_SDK_CAN_USE_TOOL_SHADOWED</code> code.</span>
<span data-as="p">This page focuses on <strong>allow and deny rules</strong> and <strong>permission modes</strong>. For the other steps:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Hooks:</strong> run custom code to allow, deny, or modify tool requests. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">Control execution with hooks</a>.</li>
<li><strong>canUseTool callback:</strong> prompt users for approval at runtime, when no earlier step resolves the call. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input" class="link">Handle approvals and user input</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="allow-and-deny-rules" aria-labelledby="_R_r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#allow-and-deny-rules" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Allow and deny rules"></a><span id="_R_r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Allow and deny rules</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">When you set <code>allowedTools</code> and <code>disallowedTools</code> (TypeScript) or <code>allowed_tools</code> and <code>disallowed_tools</code> (Python), Claude Code adds their entries to the allow and deny rule lists in the <a href="#how-permissions-are-evaluated" class="link">permission evaluation flow</a>. Any other tool not listed in <code>allowed_tools</code> is still available to Claude, and a call to it that needs approval falls through to the permission mode.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_v9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Option</th><th style="text-align:left">Effect</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>allowed_tools=[&quot;Read&quot;, &quot;Grep&quot;]</code></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>Read</code> and <code>Grep</code> are auto-approved, apart from <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">reads from network paths</a>. Other tools not listed here still exist, and calls to them that need approval fall through to the permission mode and <code>canUseTool</code>.</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>disallowed_tools=[&quot;Bash&quot;]</code></td><td style="text-align:left">The <code>Bash</code> tool definition is removed from the request.</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>disallowed_tools=[&quot;Bash(rm *)&quot;]</code></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>Bash</code> stays available. Calls matching <code>rm *</code> <a href="/docs/en/permissions#bash-rule-limits" class="link">as written</a> are denied in every permission mode, including <code>bypassPermissions</code>. Other <code>Bash</code> calls, including <code>/bin/rm</code>, fall through to the permission mode.</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>disallowed_tools=[&quot;*&quot;]</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Tool-name globs are supported in deny rules: <code>&quot;*&quot;</code> matches every tool and <code>&quot;mcp__*&quot;</code> matches every MCP tool across all servers.</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Allow rules accept tool-name globs only after a literal <code>mcp__&lt;server&gt;__</code> prefix. The server segment must be glob-free so the rule names a specific server you configured: <code>mcp__puppeteer__*</code> matches every tool from the <code>puppeteer</code> server, and <code>mcp__github__get_*</code> matches its <code>get_</code> tools. An unanchored entry like <code>allowed_tools=[&quot;*&quot;]</code> or <code>allowed_tools=[&quot;mcp__*&quot;]</code> is ignored and doesn’t auto-approve anything.</span>
<span id="calls-an-allow-rule-never-approves"></span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p"><strong>Auto-approved tools never reach <code>canUseTool</code>.</strong> A tool call approved at any earlier step, by <code>acceptEdits</code> or <code>bypassPermissions</code>, or by an allow rule, skips your <code>canUseTool</code> callback, so permission checks you put there are silently bypassed for that tool.</span><span data-as="p">If calls to a tool in <code>allowed_tools</code> still reach your <code>canUseTool</code> callback, or are denied in <code>dontAsk</code> mode, check whether they’re <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">reads from network paths</a> or among the following calls. No allow rule approves these calls. In <code>dontAsk</code> mode, which never prompts, Claude Code denies them without calling <code>canUseTool</code>, and in the other permission modes you handle them as each item says:</span><ul>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><code>AskUserQuestion</code>, MCP tools marked <a href="/docs/en/mcp#require-approval-for-a-specific-tool" class="link"><code>_meta[&quot;anthropic/requiresUserInteraction&quot;]</code></a>, and connector tools <a href="/docs/en/mcp#organization-controls-on-connector-tools" class="link">your organization set to <code>ask</code></a>: answer them in the callback. <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input#handle-clarifying-questions" class="link">Handle clarifying questions</a> shows how to answer <code>AskUserQuestion</code>, and a connector call arrives with the reason <code>Your organization requires approval for this tool</code></span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Custom tools your application marks <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools#require-approval-for-specific-tools" class="link"><code>always_ask</code></a>: decide the call in the callback. To stop these calls arriving, <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#mcpsdkserverconfigwithinstance" class="link">change the policy mid-session</a>. In <code>auto</code>, <code>plan</code>, and <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode the call can instead be approved before the callback, in the cases listed under <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#permission-modes-that-can-approve-an-always_ask-call" class="link">Permission modes that can approve an <code>always_ask</code> call</a></span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Recognized <code>rm</code> and <code>rmdir</code> <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">critical-path removals</a>: in <code>default</code>, <code>acceptEdits</code>, and <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode, allow or deny the removal in the callback, where <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#canusetool" class="link"><code>decisionReason</code></a> starts with <code>Dangerous rm</code>. For <code>auto</code> and <code>plan</code> mode, see <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-path-removals-in-each-permission-mode" class="link">Critical-path removals in each permission mode</a></span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Bare name</strong>: an entry like <code>Read</code> or <code>mcp__github__get_issue</code> auto-approves every call to that tool apart from the <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#actions-no-mode-auto-approves" class="link">actions no mode auto-approves</a> and <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">reads from network paths</a>.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Scoped rule</strong>: an entry like <code>Bash(npm test *)</code> auto-approves calls that match it, and other <code>Bash</code> calls that need approval fall through to the permission mode.</span>
</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">To check calls that an allow rule, <code>acceptEdits</code>, or <code>bypassPermissions</code> approves, use a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link"><code>PreToolUse</code> hook</a>, which runs for those calls too. A hook deny applies even in <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">For a locked-down agent, pair <code>allowedTools</code> with <code>permissionMode: &quot;dontAsk&quot;</code>:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 dark:[.code-block:has([data-fade-overlay])_[data-floating-buttons]_&amp;]:bg-transparent" id="ask-assistant-code-block-button" aria-label="Ask Assistant" group-hover/ask-assistant-button:text-gray-500 dark:text-white/40 dark:group-hover/ask-assistant-button:text-white/60"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Sparkle"></use></svg></button></div></div><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px;font-variant-ligatures:none;height:auto;background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E" data-component-part="code-block-root" class="w-0 min-w-full max-w-full h-full dark:bg-codeblock text-sm leading-6 transition-[height] duration-300 ease-in-out code-block-background **:ring-0 **:outline-0 **:focus:ring-0 **:focus:outline-0 rounded-2xl bg-white"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_6999deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="0" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] py-3.5 px-4 focus-visible:outline-1! focus-visible:-outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-primary dark:focus-visible:outline-primary-light overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="min-w-full! h-full children:my-0! children:shadow-none! children:bg-transparent!"><div class="font-mono whitespace-pre leading-6"><pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus" style="background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E;color:#1f2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4" language="typescript"><code language="typescript" numberOfLines="4"><span class="line"><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">const</span><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#4FC1FF"> options</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> =</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">  allowedTools:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> [</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;Read&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">, </span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;Glob&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">, </span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;Grep&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">],</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">  permissionMode:</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;dontAsk&quot;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">};</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div>
<span data-as="p">The listed <code>Read</code>, <code>Glob</code>, and <code>Grep</code> calls are approved, apart from the <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#actions-no-mode-auto-approves" class="link">actions no mode auto-approves</a> and <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">reads from network paths</a>. Every other call that would prompt is denied instead, though a <a href="/docs/en/plugins/mods/overview" class="link">mod</a> you installed can still approve <a href="/docs/en/permissions#precedence-among-rules-hooks-and-mods" class="link">some of those calls</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">Calls that need no approval in <code>default</code> mode run whether or not you list them. These include <a href="/docs/en/permissions#read-only-commands" class="link">read-only Bash commands</a>, tools like <code>Agent</code> that don’t ask before running, and file reads inside your working directories. To hide a built-in tool from Claude entirely rather than deny its calls, add its bare name to <code>disallowedTools</code>.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p"><strong><code>allowed_tools</code> does not constrain <code>bypassPermissions</code>.</strong> <code>allowed_tools</code> pre-approves the tools you list. In <code>bypassPermissions</code> mode, Claude Code approves a call to an unlisted tool unless it’s one of the <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#actions-no-mode-auto-approves" class="link">actions no mode auto-approves</a>.</span><span data-as="p">If you set <code>allowed_tools=[&quot;Read&quot;]</code> with <code>permission_mode=&quot;bypassPermissions&quot;</code>, Claude Code still approves calls to tools such as <code>Bash</code>, <code>Write</code>, and <code>Edit</code>. If you need <code>bypassPermissions</code> but want specific tools blocked, use <code>disallowed_tools</code>. For the calls that still reach your <code>canUseTool</code> callback in this mode, see <a href="#bypass-permissions-mode-bypasspermissions" class="link">Bypass permissions mode</a>.</span></div></div>
<span id="rules-in-settings-files"></span>
<span data-as="p">You can also configure allow, deny, and ask rules declaratively in <code>.claude/settings.json</code>. These rules are read when the <code>project</code> setting source is enabled, which it is for default <code>query()</code> options. If you set <code>setting_sources</code> (TypeScript: <code>settingSources</code>) explicitly, include <code>&quot;project&quot;</code> for them to apply. See <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#permission-settings" class="link">Permission settings</a> for the rule syntax.</span>
<span data-as="p">For when allow rules in a project’s <code>.claude/settings.json</code> apply, see <a href="/docs/en/permissions#project-allow-rules-and-workspace-trust" class="link">Project allow rules and workspace trust</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="path-patterns-in-rules" aria-labelledby="_R_1n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#path-patterns-in-rules" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Path patterns in rules"></a><span id="_R_1n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Path patterns in rules</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Scoped rules for <code>Read</code> and <code>Edit</code> take a path pattern. <code>Edit(path)</code> rules govern all built-in tools that write files, including <code>Write</code> and <code>NotebookEdit</code>, so write a path rule for any of them as <code>Edit(path)</code>. If you write a path rule as <code>Write(path)</code> or <code>NotebookEdit(path)</code>, it <a href="/docs/en/permissions#path-rules-that-never-match" class="link">matches no call</a> and has no effect.</span>
<span data-as="p">In <code>allowed_tools</code> and <code>disallowed_tools</code>, start a path pattern with <code>//</code> for an absolute path, or with a single <code>/</code> for a path inside the session’s working directory:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong><code>//path</code></strong>: with a deny rule of <code>Edit(//secrets/**)</code>, Claude Code blocks writes under <code>/secrets</code> on disk from Claude’s file tools and from <a href="/docs/en/permissions#what-read-and-edit-rules-cover" class="link">Bash commands such as <code>tee</code> and <code>&gt; file</code> redirection</a>. A script that Claude runs and that opens files itself can still write under <code>/secrets</code>. For enforcement by the operating system, see <a href="/docs/en/sandboxing" class="link">Configure the sandboxed Bash tool</a>.</li>
<li><strong><code>/path</code></strong>: <code>Edit(/secrets/**)</code> matches the <code>secrets</code> directory inside the working directory.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">For other ways to start a path pattern, see <a href="/docs/en/permissions#read-and-edit" class="link">Read and Edit rules</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="permission-modes" aria-labelledby="_R_219deehkd74t9_"><a href="#permission-modes" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Permission modes"></a><span id="_R_219deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Permission modes</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Permission modes provide global control over how Claude uses tools. You can set the permission mode when calling <code>query()</code> or change it dynamically during streaming sessions.</span>
<span data-as="p">With Claude Code v2.1.302 or later, your app starts in <code>default</code> mode unless you set a permission mode or it gets one from <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#permissions-defaultmode" class="link"><code>permissions.defaultMode</code></a> in its settings files. In <code>default</code> mode, Claude Code denies any tool call needing approval unless you approve it through an allow rule, hook, <code>canUseTool</code> callback, or permission prompt tool. If tool calls are denied, see <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#which-mode-a-non-interactive-run-starts-in" class="link">Tool calls denied in <code>claude -p</code> and Agent SDK runs</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="available-modes" aria-labelledby="_R_279deehkd74t9_"><a href="#available-modes" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Available modes"></a><span id="_R_279deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Available modes</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The SDK supports these permission modes:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_2b9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Mode</th><th style="text-align:left">Description</th><th style="text-align:left">Tool behavior</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>default</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Standard permission behavior</td><td style="text-align:left">No mode-based auto-approvals; calls that need approval and match no allow rule trigger your <code>canUseTool</code> callback</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>dontAsk</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Deny instead of prompting</td><td style="text-align:left">Any call that would otherwise prompt is denied without calling <code>canUseTool</code>, though a <a href="/docs/en/plugins/mods/overview" class="link">mod</a> you installed can still approve <a href="/docs/en/permissions#precedence-among-rules-hooks-and-mods" class="link">some of those calls</a>. Calls approved by <code>allowed_tools</code> or <code>settings.json</code> allow rules run, and so do calls that need no approval in <code>default</code> mode. Some calls, such as <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">reads from network paths</a>, are denied even when <code>allowed_tools</code> or an allow rule matches them. For the list, see <a href="#don%E2%80%99t-ask-mode-dontask" class="link">Don’t ask mode</a></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>acceptEdits</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Auto-accept file edits</td><td style="text-align:left">File edits and <a href="#accept-edits-mode-acceptedits" class="link">filesystem operations</a> (<code>mkdir</code>, <code>rm</code>, <code>mv</code>, etc.) inside your <a href="/docs/en/permissions#working-directories" class="link">working directories</a> are automatically approved</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>bypassPermissions</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Bypass permission checks</td><td style="text-align:left">Tools run without permission prompts, except for the <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#actions-no-mode-auto-approves" class="link">actions no mode auto-approves</a> and, outside a <a href="/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web" class="link">cloud session</a>, custom tools your application <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools#require-approval-for-specific-tools" class="link">marks <code>always_ask</code></a>. Use with caution</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>plan</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Planning mode</td><td style="text-align:left">Claude explores and plans. Edits made with Claude’s file tools go to your <code>canUseTool</code> callback even when an allow rule matches them</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>auto</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Model-classified approvals</td><td style="text-align:left">A model classifier reviews actions such as shell commands and network requests, allowing or blocking each one it reviews. See <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#eliminate-prompts-with-auto-mode" class="link">Auto mode</a> for availability and the decision order</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p"><strong>Subagent inheritance:</strong> If your main session is in <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#analyze-before-you-edit-with-plan-mode" class="link">plan mode</a>, a subagent whose <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#agentdefinition" class="link"><code>AgentDefinition</code></a> sets <code>permissionMode: &quot;acceptEdits&quot;</code> still runs in plan mode. That subagent’s <code>Edit</code> and <code>Write</code> calls on your source files still go to your <code>canUseTool</code> callback. Plan mode holds for the subagents you define too, so that handing work to one doesn’t get around reviewing the plan first.</span><span data-as="p">The subagent inherits <code>plan</code>, <code>acceptEdits</code>, <code>auto</code>, or <code>bypassPermissions</code> from the main session, and under a <code>default</code>- or <code>dontAsk</code>-mode main session it runs in the mode you set. Even then, Claude Code never applies a <code>&quot;bypassPermissions&quot;</code> value, and applies an <code>&quot;auto&quot;</code> value only when <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#eliminate-prompts-with-auto-mode" class="link">auto mode is available</a> to that subagent. The <code>bypassPermissions</code> exception requires Claude Code v2.1.267 or later.</span><span data-as="p">Subagents may have different system prompts and less constrained behavior than your main agent, so inheriting <code>bypassPermissions</code> grants them full, autonomous system access. The <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#actions-no-mode-auto-approves" class="link">actions no mode auto-approves</a> still apply.</span></div></div>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="set-permission-mode" aria-labelledby="_R_2f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#set-permission-mode" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Set permission mode"></a><span id="_R_2f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Set permission mode</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">You can set the permission mode once when starting a query, or change it dynamically while the session is active.</span>

<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="mode-details" aria-labelledby="_R_2l9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#mode-details" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Mode details"></a><span id="_R_2l9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Mode details</span></h3>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="accept-edits-mode-acceptedits" aria-labelledby="_R_2n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#accept-edits-mode-acceptedits" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Accept edits mode (acceptEdits)"></a><span id="_R_2n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Accept edits mode (<code>acceptEdits</code>)</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Auto-approves file operations so Claude can edit code without prompting. Other tools (like Bash commands that aren’t filesystem operations) still require normal permissions.</span>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Auto-approved operations:</strong></span>
<ul>
<li>File edits with the Edit, Write, and NotebookEdit tools</li>
<li>Filesystem commands: <code>mkdir</code>, <code>touch</code>, <code>rm</code>, <code>rmdir</code>, <code>mv</code>, <code>cp</code>, <code>sed</code></li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Both apply to paths inside the working directory or a directory you add. In Python, add one with <code>add_dirs</code>, and in TypeScript with <code>additionalDirectories</code>. In <code>acceptEdits</code> mode, Claude Code doesn’t auto-approve the request when Claude:</span>
<ul>
<li>Works on a path outside that scope</li>
<li>Writes to a <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#protected-paths" class="link">protected path</a></li>
<li>Removes a path that Claude Code recognizes as a <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">critical path</a> with <code>rm</code> or <code>rmdir</code></li>
<li>Runs <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#auto-approve-file-edits-with-acceptedits-mode" class="link"><code>touch</code></a> with, for example, an option, a glob, or a <code>timeout 5</code> or <code>LANG=C</code> prefix</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Use when:</strong> you trust Claude’s edits and want faster iteration, such as during prototyping or when working in an isolated directory.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="don’t-ask-mode-dontask" aria-labelledby="_R_359deehkd74t9_"><a href="#don’t-ask-mode-dontask" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Don’t ask mode (dontAsk)"></a><span id="_R_359deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Don’t ask mode (<code>dontAsk</code>)</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Converts any permission prompt into a denial, without calling <code>canUseTool</code>, though a <a href="/docs/en/plugins/mods/overview" class="link">mod</a> you installed can still approve <a href="/docs/en/permissions#precedence-among-rules-hooks-and-mods" class="link">some of those calls</a>. Tools pre-approved by <code>allowed_tools</code>, <code>settings.json</code> allow rules, or a hook run as normal. Calls that need no approval in <code>default</code> mode also run as normal, such as file reads inside your working directories and calls to <code>Agent</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">These calls are denied even when an allow rule matches:</span>
<ul>
<li>Connector tools <a href="/docs/en/mcp#organization-controls-on-connector-tools" class="link">your organization set to <code>ask</code></a></li>
<li>Tools that require user interaction, such as <code>AskUserQuestion</code> and MCP tools whose server sets <a href="/docs/en/mcp#require-approval-for-a-specific-tool" class="link"><code>_meta[&quot;anthropic/requiresUserInteraction&quot;]</code></a></li>
<li>Custom tools whose <code>permission_policy</code> your application sets to <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools#require-approval-for-specific-tools" class="link"><code>always_ask</code></a></li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">Reads from network paths</a></li>
<li>Recognized <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">critical-path removals</a></li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">If your <code>PreToolUse</code> hook returns <code>allow</code> for a recognized critical-path removal, a read from a network path, or a call to a tool marked <code>always_ask</code>, the call is still denied. The check that recognizes these removals <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">can miss some commands</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Use when:</strong> you run a headless agent and want a hard deny for each call that would otherwise prompt.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="bypass-permissions-mode-bypasspermissions" aria-labelledby="_R_3h9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#bypass-permissions-mode-bypasspermissions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Bypass permissions mode (bypassPermissions)"></a><span id="_R_3h9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Bypass permissions mode (<code>bypassPermissions</code>)</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Auto-approves tool uses without prompting, except the <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#actions-no-mode-auto-approves" class="link">actions no mode auto-approves</a>. Hooks still execute and can block operations if needed. On Linux and macOS, Claude Code refuses to start in this mode as root or under <code>sudo</code> outside a <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#skip-all-checks-with-bypasspermissions-mode" class="link">recognized sandbox</a>, and the query fails before the first turn.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">Use with extreme caution. Claude has full system access in this mode. Only use in controlled environments where you trust all possible operations.</span><span data-as="p"><code>allowed_tools</code> doesn’t constrain this mode, and calls to tools you didn’t list are approved too. These controls still apply:</span><ul>
<li>Deny rules, explicit <code>ask</code> rules, and hooks are evaluated before the mode check and can still block a tool.</li>
<li>Connector tools <a href="/docs/en/mcp#organization-controls-on-connector-tools" class="link">your organization set to <code>ask</code></a>, tools that require user interaction, recognized <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">critical-path removals</a>, and Bash commands flagged <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#dangerous-rm-operation-not-ruled-out" class="link"><code>Dangerous rm operation not ruled out</code></a> still fall through to your <code>canUseTool</code> callback. The check that recognizes critical-path removals <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">can miss some commands</a>, and you can <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#turn-off-the-critical-path-removal-prompt-in-bypasspermissions-mode" class="link">turn off the critical-path removal prompt</a>. Outside a <a href="/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web" class="link">cloud session</a>, calls to custom tools your application <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools#require-approval-for-specific-tools" class="link">marks <code>always_ask</code></a> fall through to the callback too.</li>
<li>The <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#skip-all-checks-with-bypasspermissions-mode" class="link">cross-session messaging safeguards</a> still apply.</li>
</ul></div></div>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="plan-mode-plan" aria-labelledby="_R_3n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#plan-mode-plan" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Plan mode (plan)"></a><span id="_R_3n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Plan mode (<code>plan</code>)</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Claude explores the codebase and produces a plan. During planning, edits made with Claude’s file tools outside <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#protected-paths" class="link">protected paths</a> reach your <code>canUseTool</code> callback, even when an allow rule matches or a <code>PreToolUse</code> hook returns <code>allow</code>. <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input#handle-clarifying-questions" class="link"><code>AskUserQuestion</code></a> calls reach your callback too, even when an allow rule names the tool.</span>
<span data-as="p">When <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#eliminate-prompts-with-auto-mode" class="link">auto mode</a> is available, Claude Code sends these calls during planning to <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#analyze-before-you-edit-with-plan-mode" class="link">auto mode’s classifier</a> for review instead of to your callback, when no allow rule approves them:</span>
<ul>
<li>Shell commands</li>
<li>Web fetches</li>
<li>Web searches</li>
<li>Calls to MCP tools that their server marks read-only</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">This review is on by default. To turn it off, set <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#useautomodeduringplan" class="link"><code>useAutoModeDuringPlan</code></a> to <code>false</code> in the <code>settings</code> option you pass to <code>query()</code>, or in your user settings. While the review is off, web searches and calls to MCP tools marked read-only reach your callback.</span>
<span data-as="p">For shell commands and web fetches, the table shows whether each call reaches your callback:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_439deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Call during planning</th><th style="text-align:left">Review on</th><th style="text-align:left">Review off, or auto mode not available</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left">A <a href="/docs/en/permissions#read-only-commands" class="link">read-only</a> shell command, such as <code>ls</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Runs without reaching your callback</td><td style="text-align:left">Runs without reaching your callback</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Any other shell command that no allow rule approves, except a <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#critical-paths" class="link">critical-path removal</a></td><td style="text-align:left">Goes to the classifier, which approves or refuses it without your callback</td><td style="text-align:left">Reaches your callback</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">A <code>WebFetch</code> call to a <a href="/docs/en/tools-reference#webfetch-tool-behavior" class="link">preapproved documentation domain</a></td><td style="text-align:left">Runs without reaching your callback</td><td style="text-align:left">Runs without reaching your callback</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Any other <code>WebFetch</code> call that no allow rule approves</td><td style="text-align:left">Goes to the classifier, which approves or refuses it without your callback</td><td style="text-align:left">Reaches your callback</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">For what happens after the classifier refuses several calls in a row, see <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#repeated-block-thresholds" class="link">Repeated-block thresholds</a>.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">In the TypeScript SDK, if you set <code>allowDangerouslySkipPermissions: true</code> alongside <code>permissionMode: &#x27;plan&#x27;</code>, file edits still reach your <code>canUseTool</code> callback. To switch a session that starts in plan mode to <code>bypassPermissions</code> later with <code>setPermissionMode()</code>, you need <code>allowDangerouslySkipPermissions</code> set from the start.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Use when:</strong> you want Claude to produce a plan first, such as during code review or before a large change.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="auto-mode-auto" aria-labelledby="_R_4b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#auto-mode-auto" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Auto mode (auto)"></a><span id="_R_4b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Auto mode (<code>auto</code>)</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">A classifier model reviews actions such as shell commands and network requests before they run, and allows or blocks each one it reviews. For the models and providers auto mode requires and the order it evaluates actions in, see <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#eliminate-prompts-with-auto-mode" class="link">Eliminate permission prompts with auto mode</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">If you switch a session that’s in auto mode to a model that auto mode doesn’t support, using <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudesdkclient" class="link"><code>set_model()</code></a>, <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#query-object" class="link"><code>setModel()</code></a>, or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#applyflagsettings" class="link"><code>applyFlagSettings()</code></a>, the session changes to <code>default</code> mode:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>What you see</strong>: a <code>system</code> message with subtype <code>status</code> and <code>permissionMode</code> set to <code>&quot;default&quot;</code>, which the TypeScript SDK types as <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkstatusmessage" class="link"><code>SDKStatusMessage</code></a>. From then on, calls that need approval and match no allow rule reach your <code>canUseTool</code> callback.</li>
<li><strong>To return to auto mode</strong>: switch to a supported model, then call <code>set_permission_mode(&quot;auto&quot;)</code> or <code>setPermissionMode(&quot;auto&quot;)</code>. If you switch the model back without making that call, the session stays in <code>default</code> mode. While the unsupported model is active, the call fails with <code>Cannot set permission mode to auto: auto mode unavailable for this model</code>.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The change to <code>default</code> mode after a <code>set_model()</code>, <code>setModel()</code>, or <code>applyFlagSettings()</code> call requires Claude Code v2.1.299 or later.</span>

<span data-as="p">For the other steps in the permission evaluation flow:</span>
<ul>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input" class="link">Handle approvals and user input</a>: interactive approval prompts and clarifying questions</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">Hooks guide</a>: run custom code at key points in the agent lifecycle</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#permission-settings" class="link">Permission rules</a>: declarative allow/deny rules in <code>settings.json</code></li>
</ul></div><nav id="pagination" data-layout-item="pagination" aria-label="Pagination" class="px-0.5 flex items-center gap-6 text-sm font-semibold text-gray-700 dark:text-gray-200"><a 150ms;\u0026:hover{color:hsl(var(--text-100));background-color:hsl(var(--bg-400));border-color:hsl(var(--bg-400))}}\u0026.ghost{color:hsl(var(--text-200));border-color:transparent;transition:color 150ms, background-color 150ms;\u0026:hover{color:hsl(var(--text-100));background-color:hsl(var(--bg-500) / 0.4)}\u0026:active{background-color:hsl(var(--bg-400))}}\u0026.underline{opacity:0.8;text-decoration-line:none;text-underline-offset:3px;transition:all 150ms;\u0026:hover{opacity:1;text-decoration-line:underline}\u0026:active{transform:scale(0.985)}}\u0026.danger{font-weight:600;color:hsl(var(--oncolor-100));background-color:hsl(var(--danger-100));transition:background-color
